I’ve been kind of antsy. If I sit too long, I start looking for something to do—messing with route plans, checking in on newer drivers after hours, fixing stuff around the house. It’s not like I’m trying to make a big deal of it, but I don’t settle well lately.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if these feelings of being distracted and restless have happened in different settings, like at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been in more than one place. My wife and daughter seem like they’re kind of walking on eggshells around me, and I know I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that sometimes you get really absorbed or hyperfocused on certain activities or interests, even if they’re not important, while other times you can’t focus at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sort of. That’s when I miss things I normally wouldn’t, like a delivery note or who I said I’d call back.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more worried, tense, or on edge than usual, even when there isn’t a clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not exactly. I’m not trying to cut people off or anything. If it happens, it’s probably just once in a while.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if these issues with focus and restlessness have been consistent throughout your adult life, or if they’ve come and gone in certain periods?\n\nPatient: It hasn’t really been my whole adult life, no. Then I miss stuff I usually wouldn’t, like a delivery note or who I said I’d call back.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself acting on impulse or feeling unusually restless or fidgety during these times when you’re having trouble focusing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I’ve been kind of antsy. If I sit too long, I start looking for something to do—messing with route plans, checking in on newer drivers after hours, fixing stuff around the house. It’s not like I’m trying to make a big deal of it, but I don’t settle well lately.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if these feelings of being distracted and restless have happened in different settings, like at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been in more than one place. At work, I’ve had to ask dispatch or clients to repeat details I should’ve caught the first time. A couple sales follow-ups slipped by too, just because I was chasing too many things in my head.\n\nAt home… it’s there too. My wife and daughter seem like they’re kind of walking on eggshells around me, and I know I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that sometimes you get really absorbed or hyperfocused on certain activities or interests, even if they’re not important, while other times you can’t focus at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sort of. It’s less like I’m locked into one hobby or anything, more that my mind grabs onto whatever pops up — a CB chirp, a fuel price sign, some new idea — and then I lose the thread of what I’m supposed to be doing. That’s when I miss things I normally wouldn’t, like a delivery note or who I said I’d call back.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more worried, tense, or on edge than usual, even when there isn’t a clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not exactly. I mean, I get tense here and there, but I haven’t really noticed it being for no reason. Hard to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even things like work, hobbies, or spending time with family?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I have off days like anybody. But I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling hopeless or losing interest in everything. I still do what I need to do.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, were you often easily distracted, restless, or impulsive as a child, or did these behaviors mostly start in adulthood?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember it being a childhood thing. Seems more like… the last seven months or so, since my daughter moved back home and I’ve been trying to keep things steady.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had times when your mind just doesn’t seem to stay on track, even when you’re trying hard to focus, like during important conversations or while reading something that needs your attention?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the main thing that got me to come in. Lately my mind just jumps around—CB goes off, I see a fuel price sign, or I get some new thought in my head, and I lose the thread. I’ve missed little details I normally wouldn’t, like a delivery note or who I was supposed to call back.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself doing things very quickly without thinking them through, or interrupting others without meaning to, more often than before?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe a little, but I haven’t really noticed that being a big thing. I’m not trying to cut people off or anything. If it happens, it’s probably just once in a while.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if these issues with focus and restlessness have been consistent throughout your adult life, or if they’ve come and gone in certain periods?\n\nPatient: It hasn’t really been my whole adult life, no. It’s more… these past seven months or so, since my daughter moved back home and I’ve been trying to keep everything steady.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to stay focused during tasks that require you to keep your attention, like planning routes, following conversations, or reading something important?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s… pretty much what I mean. When I’m trying to plan a run or keep track of what someone’s saying, my attention just snaps off to something stupid — CB noise, a fuel sign, some random idea. Then I realize I missed a detail, like a delivery note or who I said I’d call back. It’s not like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it hard to stay seated when you're supposed to, or do you feel like you're constantly 'on the go' as if driven by a motor inside you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I’ve been more on the go than usual. I keep making extra work for myself — messing with route plans again, checking in on newer drivers after hours, fixing little things at home. If I sit too long, I get kind of antsy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself interrupting others more often during conversations, or finishing their sentences before they’ve had a chance to speak, even if you don’t mean to?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, maybe I cut in once in a while like anybody, but I don’t think I’m finishing people’s sentences or anything. At least no one’s said that to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess... mostly I’m here because I haven’t been able to keep my mind on things like I usually do. Over the last few months, little stuff keeps pulling me off—CB chatter, fuel price signs, random ideas popping in—and then I miss things I normally wouldn’t, like a delivery note or who I was supposed to call back. It’s been causing problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble with distractions or staying focused for most of your life, or has this mostly been a recent issue over the last few months?\n\nPatient: Mostly recent. I mean, I’ve had normal distractions like anyone, but what got me in here is these last few months... my mind keeps jumping to every CB chirp, fuel price sign, or whatever idea pops in. Then I miss stuff I usually wouldn’t, like a delivery note or who I said I’d call back.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself acting on impulse or feeling unusually restless or fidgety during these times when you’re having trouble focusing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I’ve been kind of antsy. If I sit too long, I start looking for something to do—messing with route plans, checking in on newer drivers after hours, fixing stuff around the house. It’s not like I’m trying to make a big deal of it, but I don’t settle well lately.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if these feelings of being distracted and restless have happened in different settings, like at work, at home, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been in more than one place. At work, I’ve had to ask dispatch or clients to repeat details I should’ve caught the first time. A couple sales follow-ups slipped by too, just because I was chasing too many things in my head.\n\nAt home… it’s there too. My wife and daughter seem like they’re kind of walking on eggshells around me, and I know I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that sometimes you get really absorbed or hyperfocused on certain activities or interests, even if they’re not important, while other times you can’t focus at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sort of. It’s less like I’m locked into one hobby or anything, more that my mind grabs onto whatever pops up — a CB chirp, a fuel price sign, some new idea — and then I lose the thread of what I’m supposed to be doing. That’s when I miss things I normally wouldn’t, like a delivery note or who I said I’d call back.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more worried, tense, or on edge than usual, even when there isn’t a clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not exactly. I mean, I get tense here and there, but I haven’t really noticed it being for no reason. Hard to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even things like work, hobbies, or spending time with family?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I have off days like anybody. But I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling hopeless or losing interest in everything. I still do what I need to do.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, were you often easily distracted, restless, or impulsive as a child, or did these behaviors mostly start in adulthood?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember it being a childhood thing. Seems more like… the last seven months or so, since my daughter moved back home and I’ve been trying to keep things steady.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had times when your mind just doesn’t seem to stay on track, even when you’re trying hard to focus, like during important conversations or while reading something that needs your attention?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the main thing that got me to come in. Lately my mind just jumps around—CB goes off, I see a fuel price sign, or I get some new thought in my head, and I lose the thread. I’ve missed little details I normally wouldn’t, like a delivery note or who I was supposed to call back.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself doing things very quickly without thinking them through, or interrupting others without meaning to, more often than before?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe a little, but I haven’t really noticed that being a big thing. I’m not trying to cut people off or anything. If it happens, it’s probably just once in a while.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if these issues with focus and restlessness have been consistent throughout your adult life, or if they’ve come and gone in certain periods?\n\nPatient: It hasn’t really been my whole adult life, no. It’s not like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it hard to stay seated when you're supposed to, or do you feel like you're constantly 'on the go' as if driven by a motor inside you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I’ve been more on the go than usual. If I sit too long, I get kind of antsy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself interrupting others more often during conversations, or finishing their sentences before they’ve had a chance to speak, even if you don’t mean to?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.
